- **Assessment:** The 273 `has no chunk holder for reading chunk` warnings at 20:09:52–20:09:53 are consistent with Moonrise entering MultiPaper's no-holder fallback. `readRegionFileAsync` sends `ForceReadChunkMessage` to Master; Master reads its authoritative region storage through `RegionFileCache` and waits for the chunk write lock. This fallback does not read backend-local region storage. The warning text alone is not evidence of an authority bypass, stale data, or chest loss; stale/missing Master state from an earlier ordering defect remains possible and is not ruled out.
- **Data-integrity evidence:** Existing isolated Java 25 stress evidence exercised 273 concurrent production-path reads with populated single/double chests and an announced pending write; exact stacks survived chunk load and graceful backend/Master restarts (`performance-results/rc5-crafty1-273-noholder-read-stress-20261028.md`). Separate exact rebuilt-Master A→B and B→A handoff runs also passed with 273 reads, delayed stale former-owner writes rejected, and exact single/double chest contents preserved across owner and Master restarts (results and hashes above). These are selected synthetic schedules, not a reproduction of the live incident or proof of recovery.
- **Correlated signals:** Historical log analysis records 119 movement warnings (16:27:01–16:27:07), 8 duplicate UUID registration warnings, 13 entity NBT updates before entity chunks were available, 10 failed non-owner entity-removal routes with fallback, and 3 rejected stale inventory replacements. These do not establish chest loss. The three stale replacements are not item-conservation verification; RC5-02 remains P0 `BLOCKED`.
- **Status:** No new defect or source change is justified by the warning count alone. RC5-01 stays P0 `TESTING`; live chest-loss attribution and any recovery remain unverified. RC5-13 entity synchronization status remains as recorded in its issue entry. No live files were read or changed, no restoration was attempted, and no tests were rerun in this continuation because the exact new log was unavailable and the retained hash-pinned schedules have no contradictory evidence.

## RC5 beta monitor interval CPU sampling — 2026-10-29

The collector used to instantiate a new `psutil.Process` before every interval-free CPU percentage query, making its own CPU field an uninitialized 0% baseline; host CPU was also sampled separately for each service on PC1. Monitor v1.1.0 now retains/warm-starts the collector process sampler and shares a single host/collector CPU snapshot across local services per poll; Java process CPU/RSS remains per configured service. Added deterministic regression coverage. Python monitor suite: **17/17 passed**, `py_compile` passed, version reports `1.1.0`, and scoped `git diff --check` passed. Hashes and scope: `performance-results/rc5-monitor-interval-cpu-sampling-20261029.md`. Synthetic-only evidence: no live Crafty overhead or target-PC capacity was measured. RC5-01 and RC5-02 remain P0 gates.

- End-to-end local smoke supplement: v1.1.0 wrote two synthetic service records sharing identical host/collector interval metrics; absent RCON and Java processes remained null, disk fields were populated, and the collector exited with no process/listener left. One sample is not an overhead or target-Crafty measurement; see the evidence report.

### RC5-02 non-owner main inventory snapshot authority guard — 2026-10-29

- **Source finding:** `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacket.handle()` already checks the target UUID/session and declared owner, but `isUpdateFromPlayerOwner` previously allowed any connected peer to publish the `items` component; only `enderchest` required source backend == owner backend. This creates a plausible peer-mirror overwrite path if a plugin mutation marks an `ExternalPlayer` mirror dirty and emits a snapshot. The retained three stale replacement warnings do not prove this path caused duplication or loss.
- **Change under validation:** The helper now requires the source connection backend to match the packet's declared current owner for every inventory component. The packet-handler regression sends a main-inventory snapshot from backend-b for a backend-a-owned current session and asserts there is no inventory update or equipment detection. Helper coverage asserts owner acceptance and non-owner/unknown-source rejection for main and Ender Chest inventories.
- **Compatibility boundary:** Cross-owner plugin-driven inventory mutation/update is now rejected unless a future durable owner-attested transaction protocol is implemented. Owner-origin updates remain allowed. This does not provide InvSee++ cross-owner item transfer; remote clicks remain fail-closed.
- **Verification/status:** Source and regression are changed but the focused test, full suite, and clean builds have not passed in this continuation. The dedicated detached worktree checkout is being prepared for isolation and is unusually slow on this volume. Do not treat the code change as VERIFIED or as the root cause of the historical warnings. RC5-02 stays P0 `BLOCKED` pending reproducible cross-owner item-conservation tests and owner transaction support; the mitigation itself is `TESTING`.
- **Live safety:** No Crafty process, server, world, inventory, or player data was accessed or changed.
- **Checkout note:** The dedicated detached worktree checkout was stopped at 16% after about four minutes because it was progressing only about 50 files per 30 seconds. Its partial folder and Git metadata were retained; no cleanup was run. No isolated tests/builds were completed.
- **Current candidate hashes:** `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacket.java` SHA-256 `9B25EF1EC2B67160D6E7E26A4CDA37327543927C79350BCE9275578B00A31C04`; `PlayerInventoryUpdatePacketTest.java` SHA-256 `9227EE38DD01A5DBF6416656BA94CE342DFF3403D6CB3B4A97F21493B2F62C57`. `git diff --check` passed for these scoped edits and tracker.

### RC5-02 owner-source inventory guard red/green regression — 2026-10-29

- **Reproduction:** On an isolated Java 25.0.3.9 / Gradle 9.8 fixture, the regression models a backend-b packet addressed to backend-a's real `ServerPlayer` while the current UUID/session and declared owner are backend-a. Against the original helper (which exempted main `items` from owner-source checks), the focused suite failed **2/10**: helper accepted the peer snapshot, and packet handling called `MultiPaperInventoryHandler.updateInventory`. Red JUnit XML SHA-256 `261ED9C513166EC51619B0EEFDF9F8593951A7EDF0C20546DF7E58EA70EAFC7D`; red console log SHA-256 `A2C9E162B7D51E7A7197289A87FE74AD2D1A61882A8FE30500C7EB1038D3CE07`, both at `D:\multipaper-tests\rc5-inventory-owner-red-20261029.{xml,log}`.
- **Fix/green:** Requiring source backend == current owner for all components makes the same focused suite pass **10/10**, 0 failures/errors/skips. The tests cover owner/non-owner/unknown source for items and Ender Chest, and the packet handler's non-owner main-inventory rejection without mutation. Green XML SHA-256 `5B41089802402967F9132D261DDF827F4063B287D1DA4292EAAC4AA14509485A` at `D:\multipaper-tests\rc5-inventory-owner-green-20261029.xml`.

The baseline packet, `MultiPaperInventoryHandler`, and `PlayerSyncSession` source files are byte-identical between those commits. The fixture already had unrelated edits to `MultiPaper.java`, `MultiPaperConnection.java`, and `MultiPaperInventoryHandlerTestSuite.java`; those were left untouched. Paperweight's Purpur fetch task was excluded while using existing generated inputs. This is focused regression evidence, not a clean full build.
- **Causality and scope:** Caller tracing shows dirty `ExternalPlayer` mirror components can broadcast snapshots to subscribed backends. The regression proves the packet layer accepted a peer after-image for the owner's real player before the guard. It does **not** reproduce the reported InvSee++ click sequence or prove that this was the source of the three retained stale-replacement warnings or item duplication. The guard may disable remote plugin/API mirror mutations; owner-origin updates remain accepted. Cross-owner InvSee++ transfer still needs durable owner-attested transaction and repeated item-conservation coverage.

### RC5-01 forced claim late-save barrier TOCTOU — 2026-10-29

- **New reproduced race:** The prior active-owner guard did not atomically couple the async empty-barrier observation to later ownership promotion. `WillSaveChunkLater` can register after `waitForLockAsync` sees empty but before `forceLockIfNoOtherOwner` promotes a chunk. Its later chunk write can then be rejected as a former-owner write. The earlier claim that the active-owner regression closed this distinct empty-barrier interval is superseded.
- **Fix/regression:** The claim helper now rechecks `ChunkLockManager.hasPendingWrites` while holding the same ordered chunk stripes used by save registration. The new isolated chest regression failed before the change (claim incorrectly returned true) and passed after: final diamond x37 chunk bytes were read back exactly after the save completed and ownership retry succeeded. Focused Java 25.0.3.9 / Gradle 9.8 result: **1/1 passed**. Red/green XML and log hashes, source hashes, and limitations: `performance-results/rc5-forced-claim-late-save-barrier-20261029.md`.
- **Broader validation:** This fixture’s full Master test attempt excluded `shadowJar`, causing all 8 standalone-Master restart tests to fail to start; a saved-data integration test also timed out in the 99-test run and in the 45-test integration-class run, but passed when rerun alone (1/1). The new regression passed in the integration-class run. These are not full-suite passes; clean builds and Paperclip integration are still required.
- **Status:** This narrow TOCTOU regression/fix is `VERIFIED` in its focused Master helper path; RC5-01 overall stays P0 `TESTING` because the live empty-chest cause/recovery remains unknown and the exact new Crafty1 log bytes remain unavailable. No live system was touched.

**Execution note:** One intended focused rerun started from the project root by mistake and was stopped at local `compileJava` before test execution. It is excluded from evidence and may have refreshed ignored build outputs only. All reported red/green tests used the detached D: fixture; no live services were accessed.
